Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `comb_sop_2_18` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
- input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
- output ports: result[64]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: none; the block is combinational, and `result` is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.

5 call(s) in 2 passes, in order

Role: planner plan reviewer editor acceptance reviewer. A plan reviewer sending the plan back, or the editor being asked again, is a retry inside the same pass.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, stereovision1.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision1.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source and the relevant regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr.corr_out_tmp (lines 1374/1375 pair) and corr_seq.corr_out_tmp (lines 1867/1868 pair) — the two products of each module are the two product terms of one block; the wider 4-product tree completes untouched at sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (corr_out_1X/2X/4X, lines 316-410)
BLOCKS: 76 (one comb_sop_2_18 instantiation statement in module corr, elaborating to 64 blocks, and one in module corr_seq, elaborating to 12)
MAPPING: in corr: ax={10'b0, in_l_re}, ay={11'b0, in_r_re}, bx={10'b0, in_l_im}, by={11'b0, in_r_im}, result -> sop_res[63:0], of which sop_res[15:0] is used; in corr_seq: ax={10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, ay={11'b0, in_r_re_reg}, bx={10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, by={11'b0, in_r_im_reg}, result -> sop_res[63:0], of which sop_res[15:0] is used. All four product-term ports are used in both; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

What is being converted and why it is safe

All four candidates are converted. In each of the two modules the two products and the adder that joins them are exactly ax*ay + bx*by on 8-bit unsigned operands, so one block holds the whole 2-product sum.

  • Operands are reg/wire [7:0] (sh_reg_w = 8) and the multiply is unsigned in the original, matching the block's unsigned reading of its ports; zero-extension to ax/bx[18] and ay/by[19] is the same value.
  • The original per-product registers lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg are 16 bits and each 8×8 product is exact in 16 bits, so no product is truncated in the original either. The original sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is captured in the 16-bit corr_out_tmp, i.e. taken mod 2^16; taking result[15:0] of the block's exact sum gives the identical value.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are read only by the corr_out_tmp sum (corr: declared 1367-1368, used only at 1385; corr_seq: declared 1844-1845, used only at 1869). No individual product escapes either module, so both products may live inside the block. corr_out_tmp and corr_out keep their exact original values and timing, so everything above (wrapper_corr_*, wrapper_norm_corr_*, and the top-level sums at lines 316/368/400) is unaffected and is not edited.
  • In corr_seq the operand registers in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg (gated by new_data, lines 1850-1865) stay exactly as they are and feed the block; they are also read nowhere else.

Edit 1 — module corr (lines 1352-1387)

Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg (1367-1368) and the two multiply assignments (1374-1375). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),
        .result(sop_res));

and inside the existing always @(posedge clk) block put, in place of the two deleted multiply assignments:

verilog
            sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1385 to:

verilog
          corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;

Leave the new_data-gated corr_out <= corr_out_tmp (1377-1384) untouched. Leave the four unused wire declarations at 1363-1366 untouched.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = lrexrre_reg(t-1) + limxrim_reg(t-1) = (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im)(t-2) mod 2^16. New: sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = the same value. With all registers at 0 at power-up the two also agree on the first cycles: original corr_out_tmp(1) = 0 + 0 = 0, new corr_out_tmp(1) = sop_reg(0) = 0; and the new sop_reg(1) = f(inputs(0)) matches the original corr_out_tmp(2). The zero state maps to the zero state, so no start-up or post-reset divergence is introduced (corr has no reset port; rst reaches only top-level registers, so mid-run resets do not touch this pipeline).

Edit 2 — module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871)

Identical shape, but the block reads the already-registered operands. Delete lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (1844-1845) and the two multiplies (1867-1868). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re_reg}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re_reg}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im_reg}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im_reg}),
        .result(sop_res));

Inside the existing always @(posedge clk), in place of the deleted multiplies:

verilog
             sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1869 to corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;. The new_data-gated operand and corr_out assignments (1850-1865) are unchanged.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = f(in_*_reg(t-2)); new corr_out_tmp(t) = sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = f(in_*_reg(t-2)). Same zero-state argument as above.

Register stages, before and after

  • corr: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 2 stages (product registers, then the sum register). After: 2 stages (sop_reg after the combinational block, then corr_out_tmp). The further new_data-gated stage into corr_out is unchanged, so the module's input-to-corr_out latency stays 3 gated/ungated stages exactly as before.
  • corr_seq: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 3 stages (in_*_reg, product registers, sum register). After: 3 stages (in_*_reg, sop_reg, corr_out_tmp). Input-to-corr_out stays 4 stages.
  • No register changes anywhere else; the block itself has no registers and sits between two soft flip-flop stages in both modules (its inputs come from flip-flop outputs — sh_reg outputs for corr, in_*_reg for corr_seq — and its output is captured by sop_reg in the same cycle).

Registers removed/added

Per instance, two 16-bit registers are removed and one 16-bit register (sop_reg) is added: a net saving of 16 flip-flops × 76 instances. No added register's power-up value is relied upon for correctness beyond the zero-state mapping shown above, which holds for the original and the rewrite alike.

Candidates left unconverted

None. All four listed products are converted.

Resource check

76 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone, against 306 DSP tiles on the device. These are the only four multiply sites in the whole design (no other * operator exists in stereovision1.v, including in my_wrapper_divider), so nothing else competes for DSP tiles.

Instructions to the editor

  • Do not add a definition of comb_sop_2_18 to stereovision1.v; instantiate it only (the behavioural model is supplied as sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v).
  • Use the explicit zero-extending concatenations above rather than relying on implicit port widening.
  • Change nothing outside modules corr and corr_seq.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule

Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is stereovision1.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64

corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the relevant regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr.corr_out_tmp (lines 1374/1375 pair) and corr_seq.corr_out_tmp (lines 1867/1868 pair) — the two products of each module are the two product terms of one block; the wider 4-product tree completes untouched at sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (corr_out_1X/2X/4X, lines 316-410)
BLOCKS: 76 (one comb_sop_2_18 instantiation statement in module corr, elaborating to 64 blocks, and one in module corr_seq, elaborating to 12)
MAPPING: in corr: ax={10'b0, in_l_re}, ay={11'b0, in_r_re}, bx={10'b0, in_l_im}, by={11'b0, in_r_im}, result -> sop_res[63:0], of which sop_res[15:0] is used; in corr_seq: ax={10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, ay={11'b0, in_r_re_reg}, bx={10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, by={11'b0, in_r_im_reg}, result -> sop_res[63:0], of which sop_res[15:0] is used. All four product-term ports are used in both; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

What is being converted and why it is safe

All four candidates are converted. In each of the two modules the two products and the adder that joins them are exactly ax*ay + bx*by on 8-bit unsigned operands, so one block holds the whole 2-product sum.

  • Operands are reg/wire [7:0] (sh_reg_w = 8) and the multiply is unsigned in the original, matching the block's unsigned reading of its ports; zero-extension to ax/bx[18] and ay/by[19] is the same value.
  • The original per-product registers lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg are 16 bits and each 8×8 product is exact in 16 bits, so no product is truncated in the original either. The original sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is captured in the 16-bit corr_out_tmp, i.e. taken mod 2^16; taking result[15:0] of the block's exact sum gives the identical value.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are read only by the corr_out_tmp sum (corr: declared 1367-1368, used only at 1385; corr_seq: declared 1844-1845, used only at 1869). No individual product escapes either module, so both products may live inside the block. corr_out_tmp and corr_out keep their exact original values and timing, so everything above (wrapper_corr_*, wrapper_norm_corr_*, and the top-level sums at lines 316/368/400) is unaffected and is not edited.
  • In corr_seq the operand registers in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg (gated by new_data, lines 1850-1865) stay exactly as they are and feed the block; they are also read nowhere else.

Edit 1 — module corr (lines 1352-1387)

Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg (1367-1368) and the two multiply assignments (1374-1375). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),
        .result(sop_res));

and inside the existing always @(posedge clk) block put, in place of the two deleted multiply assignments:

verilog
            sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1385 to:

verilog
          corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;

Leave the new_data-gated corr_out <= corr_out_tmp (1377-1384) untouched. Leave the four unused wire declarations at 1363-1366 untouched.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = lrexrre_reg(t-1) + limxrim_reg(t-1) = (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im)(t-2) mod 2^16. New: sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = the same value. With all registers at 0 at power-up the two also agree on the first cycles: original corr_out_tmp(1) = 0 + 0 = 0, new corr_out_tmp(1) = sop_reg(0) = 0; and the new sop_reg(1) = f(inputs(0)) matches the original corr_out_tmp(2). The zero state maps to the zero state, so no start-up or post-reset divergence is introduced (corr has no reset port; rst reaches only top-level registers, so mid-run resets do not touch this pipeline).

Edit 2 — module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871)

Identical shape, but the block reads the already-registered operands. Delete lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (1844-1845) and the two multiplies (1867-1868). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re_reg}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re_reg}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im_reg}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im_reg}),
        .result(sop_res));

Inside the existing always @(posedge clk), in place of the deleted multiplies:

verilog
             sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1869 to corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;. The new_data-gated operand and corr_out assignments (1850-1865) are unchanged.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = f(in_*_reg(t-2)); new corr_out_tmp(t) = sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = f(in_*_reg(t-2)). Same zero-state argument as above.

Register stages, before and after

  • corr: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 2 stages (product registers, then the sum register). After: 2 stages (sop_reg after the combinational block, then corr_out_tmp). The further new_data-gated stage into corr_out is unchanged, so the module's input-to-corr_out latency stays 3 gated/ungated stages exactly as before.
  • corr_seq: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 3 stages (in_*_reg, product registers, sum register). After: 3 stages (in_*_reg, sop_reg, corr_out_tmp). Input-to-corr_out stays 4 stages.
  • No register changes anywhere else; the block itself has no registers and sits between two soft flip-flop stages in both modules (its inputs come from flip-flop outputs — sh_reg outputs for corr, in_*_reg for corr_seq — and its output is captured by sop_reg in the same cycle).

Registers removed/added

Per instance, two 16-bit registers are removed and one 16-bit register (sop_reg) is added: a net saving of 16 flip-flops × 76 instances. No added register's power-up value is relied upon for correctness beyond the zero-state mapping shown above, which holds for the original and the rewrite alike.

Candidates left unconverted

None. All four listed products are converted.

Resource check

76 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone, against 306 DSP tiles on the device. These are the only four multiply sites in the whole design (no other * operator exists in stereovision1.v, including in my_wrapper_divider), so nothing else competes for DSP tiles.

Instructions to the editor

  • Do not add a definition of comb_sop_2_18 to stereovision1.v; instantiate it only (the behavioural model is supplied as sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v).
  • Use the explicit zero-extending concatenations above rather than relying on implicit port widening.
  • Change nothing outside modules corr and corr_seq.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the design source now.

Let me verify the surrounding hierarchy, readers, and reset paths.

APPROVE

Checks performed against stereovision1.v:

  • Operands/widths. corr lines 1374-1375 multiply the module inputs in_l_re/in_r_re, in_l_im/in_r_im (the _reg names at 1363-1366 are undriven wires), and corr_seq lines 1867-1868 multiply the registered in_*_reg (1840-1843). The plan's MAPPING uses exactly those nets. All are [sh_reg_w-1:0] = 8 bits, unsigned; {10'b0,x}→18 and {11'b0,x}→19 match the port widths in sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v.
  • Arithmetic. Each 8×8 product ≤ 65025 fits the original 16-bit lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg exactly; the original sum at 1385/1869 truncates to 16 bits, and result[15:0] of the block's exact 64-bit sum is the same value.
  • Visibility. lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg are read only at 1385 (corr, module 1352-1387) and 1869 (corr_seq, module 1829-1871); in_*_reg in corr_seq only at 1867-1868. Nothing else reads them, so both products may live inside the block. corr_out_tmp/corr_out keep their values and timing, so wrapper_corr_*, wrapper_norm_corr_* and the top sums at 316/368/400 are unaffected.
  • Register retiming. Original corr_out_tmp(t)=f(inputs(t-2)); rewritten corr_out_tmp(t)=sop_reg(t-1)=sop_res(t-2)[15:0]=f(inputs(t-2)). Same for corr_seq with one extra new_data-gated stage in front. Stage counts stated (2→2, 3→3) are correct, and the gated corr_out assignments (1377-1384, 1850-1865) are left alone.
  • Reset. Neither corr nor corr_seq has a reset port, so there is no block reset to drive. The start-up argument is not load-bearing in practice: new_data for these instances is vidin_new_data_scld_*_2to3_left, which is 0 whenever counter_out_tmp != 3'b001 (955-1014) and counter_out_tmp is cleared by rst (≈940), so the one cycle in which sop_reg holds its power-up value cannot be captured into corr_out during the testbench's reset window; the testbench also zeroes every soft register ($init_zero, tb line 57). (The plan's aside that "rst reaches only top-level registers" is loose — rst also feeds port_bus_* and my_wrapper_divider at 2264 — but nothing on the converted path.)
  • Counts. wrapper_norm_corr_20×2 (21 corr each, 1247-1267) + wrapper_norm_corr_10×2 (11 each, 1590-1610) = 64; wrapper_norm_corr_5_seq×2 (6 each, 1734-1739) = 12. 76 blocks, ≤ 306 DSP tiles. A search confirms lines 1374, 1375, 1867, 1868 are the only * operators in the file outside 2 * sh_reg_w width expressions.
  • Required items. All four candidates listed and converted (none left), block count given, per-region register stages before and after given. No ASSUMES: is needed, and none is claimed.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit stereovision1.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem). The plan names their shared sum as 'corr.corr_out_tmp (lines 1374/1375 pair) and corr_seq.corr_out_tmp (lines 1867/1868 pair) — the two products of each module are the two product terms of one block; the wider 4-product tree completes untouched at sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (corr_out_1X/2X/4X, lines 316-410)'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh stereovision1.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the relevant regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr.corr_out_tmp (lines 1374/1375 pair) and corr_seq.corr_out_tmp (lines 1867/1868 pair) — the two products of each module are the two product terms of one block; the wider 4-product tree completes untouched at sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (corr_out_1X/2X/4X, lines 316-410)
BLOCKS: 76 (one comb_sop_2_18 instantiation statement in module corr, elaborating to 64 blocks, and one in module corr_seq, elaborating to 12)
MAPPING: in corr: ax={10'b0, in_l_re}, ay={11'b0, in_r_re}, bx={10'b0, in_l_im}, by={11'b0, in_r_im}, result -> sop_res[63:0], of which sop_res[15:0] is used; in corr_seq: ax={10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, ay={11'b0, in_r_re_reg}, bx={10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, by={11'b0, in_r_im_reg}, result -> sop_res[63:0], of which sop_res[15:0] is used. All four product-term ports are used in both; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

What is being converted and why it is safe

All four candidates are converted. In each of the two modules the two products and the adder that joins them are exactly ax*ay + bx*by on 8-bit unsigned operands, so one block holds the whole 2-product sum.

  • Operands are reg/wire [7:0] (sh_reg_w = 8) and the multiply is unsigned in the original, matching the block's unsigned reading of its ports; zero-extension to ax/bx[18] and ay/by[19] is the same value.
  • The original per-product registers lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg are 16 bits and each 8×8 product is exact in 16 bits, so no product is truncated in the original either. The original sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is captured in the 16-bit corr_out_tmp, i.e. taken mod 2^16; taking result[15:0] of the block's exact sum gives the identical value.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are read only by the corr_out_tmp sum (corr: declared 1367-1368, used only at 1385; corr_seq: declared 1844-1845, used only at 1869). No individual product escapes either module, so both products may live inside the block. corr_out_tmp and corr_out keep their exact original values and timing, so everything above (wrapper_corr_*, wrapper_norm_corr_*, and the top-level sums at lines 316/368/400) is unaffected and is not edited.
  • In corr_seq the operand registers in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg (gated by new_data, lines 1850-1865) stay exactly as they are and feed the block; they are also read nowhere else.

Edit 1 — module corr (lines 1352-1387)

Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg (1367-1368) and the two multiply assignments (1374-1375). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),
        .result(sop_res));

and inside the existing always @(posedge clk) block put, in place of the two deleted multiply assignments:

verilog
            sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1385 to:

verilog
          corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;

Leave the new_data-gated corr_out <= corr_out_tmp (1377-1384) untouched. Leave the four unused wire declarations at 1363-1366 untouched.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = lrexrre_reg(t-1) + limxrim_reg(t-1) = (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im)(t-2) mod 2^16. New: sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = the same value. With all registers at 0 at power-up the two also agree on the first cycles: original corr_out_tmp(1) = 0 + 0 = 0, new corr_out_tmp(1) = sop_reg(0) = 0; and the new sop_reg(1) = f(inputs(0)) matches the original corr_out_tmp(2). The zero state maps to the zero state, so no start-up or post-reset divergence is introduced (corr has no reset port; rst reaches only top-level registers, so mid-run resets do not touch this pipeline).

Edit 2 — module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871)

Identical shape, but the block reads the already-registered operands. Delete lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (1844-1845) and the two multiplies (1867-1868). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re_reg}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re_reg}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im_reg}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im_reg}),
        .result(sop_res));

Inside the existing always @(posedge clk), in place of the deleted multiplies:

verilog
             sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1869 to corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;. The new_data-gated operand and corr_out assignments (1850-1865) are unchanged.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = f(in_*_reg(t-2)); new corr_out_tmp(t) = sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = f(in_*_reg(t-2)). Same zero-state argument as above.

Register stages, before and after

  • corr: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 2 stages (product registers, then the sum register). After: 2 stages (sop_reg after the combinational block, then corr_out_tmp). The further new_data-gated stage into corr_out is unchanged, so the module's input-to-corr_out latency stays 3 gated/ungated stages exactly as before.
  • corr_seq: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 3 stages (in_*_reg, product registers, sum register). After: 3 stages (in_*_reg, sop_reg, corr_out_tmp). Input-to-corr_out stays 4 stages.
  • No register changes anywhere else; the block itself has no registers and sits between two soft flip-flop stages in both modules (its inputs come from flip-flop outputs — sh_reg outputs for corr, in_*_reg for corr_seq — and its output is captured by sop_reg in the same cycle).

Registers removed/added

Per instance, two 16-bit registers are removed and one 16-bit register (sop_reg) is added: a net saving of 16 flip-flops × 76 instances. No added register's power-up value is relied upon for correctness beyond the zero-state mapping shown above, which holds for the original and the rewrite alike.

Candidates left unconverted

None. All four listed products are converted.

Resource check

76 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone, against 306 DSP tiles on the device. These are the only four multiply sites in the whole design (no other * operator exists in stereovision1.v, including in my_wrapper_divider), so nothing else competes for DSP tiles.

Instructions to the editor

  • Do not add a definition of comb_sop_2_18 to stereovision1.v; instantiate it only (the behavioural model is supplied as sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v).
  • Use the explicit zero-extending concatenations above rather than relying on implicit port widening.
  • Change nothing outside modules corr and corr_seq.
Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file stereovision1.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

Output
  • First line exactly ACCEPT or REJECT.
  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the relevant regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr.corr_out_tmp (lines 1374/1375 pair) and corr_seq.corr_out_tmp (lines 1867/1868 pair) — the two products of each module are the two product terms of one block; the wider 4-product tree completes untouched at sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (corr_out_1X/2X/4X, lines 316-410)
BLOCKS: 76 (one comb_sop_2_18 instantiation statement in module corr, elaborating to 64 blocks, and one in module corr_seq, elaborating to 12)
MAPPING: in corr: ax={10'b0, in_l_re}, ay={11'b0, in_r_re}, bx={10'b0, in_l_im}, by={11'b0, in_r_im}, result -> sop_res[63:0], of which sop_res[15:0] is used; in corr_seq: ax={10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, ay={11'b0, in_r_re_reg}, bx={10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, by={11'b0, in_r_im_reg}, result -> sop_res[63:0], of which sop_res[15:0] is used. All four product-term ports are used in both; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

What is being converted and why it is safe

All four candidates are converted. In each of the two modules the two products and the adder that joins them are exactly ax*ay + bx*by on 8-bit unsigned operands, so one block holds the whole 2-product sum.

  • Operands are reg/wire [7:0] (sh_reg_w = 8) and the multiply is unsigned in the original, matching the block's unsigned reading of its ports; zero-extension to ax/bx[18] and ay/by[19] is the same value.
  • The original per-product registers lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg are 16 bits and each 8×8 product is exact in 16 bits, so no product is truncated in the original either. The original sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is captured in the 16-bit corr_out_tmp, i.e. taken mod 2^16; taking result[15:0] of the block's exact sum gives the identical value.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are read only by the corr_out_tmp sum (corr: declared 1367-1368, used only at 1385; corr_seq: declared 1844-1845, used only at 1869). No individual product escapes either module, so both products may live inside the block. corr_out_tmp and corr_out keep their exact original values and timing, so everything above (wrapper_corr_*, wrapper_norm_corr_*, and the top-level sums at lines 316/368/400) is unaffected and is not edited.
  • In corr_seq the operand registers in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg (gated by new_data, lines 1850-1865) stay exactly as they are and feed the block; they are also read nowhere else.

Edit 1 — module corr (lines 1352-1387)

Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg (1367-1368) and the two multiply assignments (1374-1375). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),
        .result(sop_res));

and inside the existing always @(posedge clk) block put, in place of the two deleted multiply assignments:

verilog
            sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1385 to:

verilog
          corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;

Leave the new_data-gated corr_out <= corr_out_tmp (1377-1384) untouched. Leave the four unused wire declarations at 1363-1366 untouched.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = lrexrre_reg(t-1) + limxrim_reg(t-1) = (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im)(t-2) mod 2^16. New: sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = the same value. With all registers at 0 at power-up the two also agree on the first cycles: original corr_out_tmp(1) = 0 + 0 = 0, new corr_out_tmp(1) = sop_reg(0) = 0; and the new sop_reg(1) = f(inputs(0)) matches the original corr_out_tmp(2). The zero state maps to the zero state, so no start-up or post-reset divergence is introduced (corr has no reset port; rst reaches only top-level registers, so mid-run resets do not touch this pipeline).

Edit 2 — module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871)

Identical shape, but the block reads the already-registered operands. Delete lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (1844-1845) and the two multiplies (1867-1868). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re_reg}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re_reg}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im_reg}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im_reg}),
        .result(sop_res));

Inside the existing always @(posedge clk), in place of the deleted multiplies:

verilog
             sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1869 to corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;. The new_data-gated operand and corr_out assignments (1850-1865) are unchanged.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = f(in_*_reg(t-2)); new corr_out_tmp(t) = sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = f(in_*_reg(t-2)). Same zero-state argument as above.

Register stages, before and after

  • corr: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 2 stages (product registers, then the sum register). After: 2 stages (sop_reg after the combinational block, then corr_out_tmp). The further new_data-gated stage into corr_out is unchanged, so the module's input-to-corr_out latency stays 3 gated/ungated stages exactly as before.
  • corr_seq: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 3 stages (in_*_reg, product registers, sum register). After: 3 stages (in_*_reg, sop_reg, corr_out_tmp). Input-to-corr_out stays 4 stages.
  • No register changes anywhere else; the block itself has no registers and sits between two soft flip-flop stages in both modules (its inputs come from flip-flop outputs — sh_reg outputs for corr, in_*_reg for corr_seq — and its output is captured by sop_reg in the same cycle).

Registers removed/added

Per instance, two 16-bit registers are removed and one 16-bit register (sop_reg) is added: a net saving of 16 flip-flops × 76 instances. No added register's power-up value is relied upon for correctness beyond the zero-state mapping shown above, which holds for the original and the rewrite alike.

Candidates left unconverted

None. All four listed products are converted.

Resource check

76 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone, against 306 DSP tiles on the device. These are the only four multiply sites in the whole design (no other * operator exists in stereovision1.v, including in my_wrapper_divider), so nothing else competes for DSP tiles.

Instructions to the editor

  • Do not add a definition of comb_sop_2_18 to stereovision1.v; instantiate it only (the behavioural model is supplied as sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v).
  • Use the explicit zero-extending concatenations above rather than relying on implicit port widening.
  • Change nothing outside modules corr and corr_seq.

Visibility. Both modules are fully visible in the diff context; lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg were read only by the corr_out_tmp sum, so no per-product value escaped. corr_out_tmp is read only by corr_out (1384/1390, 1868/1879), and corr_out's value and timing are unchanged, so everything above these modules is unaffected.

Registers. Stage count is preserved in both modules (2 stages to corr_out_tmp in corr, 3 in corr_seq); sop_reg is a 1:1 re-encoding of the removed product-register stage, not an added pipeline stage, and the invariant sop_reg = lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg holds from the first clocked update onward. Neither module has a reset port, and rst reaches only top-level registers, so mid-run reset behavior is unchanged.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule

prompt

2 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, stereovision1.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

All four product-term ports are used in both; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

What is being converted and why it is safe

All four candidates are converted. In each of the two modules the two products and the adder that joins them are exactly ax*ay + bx*by on 8-bit unsigned operands, so one block holds the whole 2-product sum.

  • Operands are reg/wire [7:0] (sh_reg_w = 8) and the multiply is unsigned in the original, matching the block's unsigned reading of its ports; zero-extension to ax/bx[18] and ay/by[19] is the same value.
  • The original per-product registers lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg are 16 bits and each 8×8 product is exact in 16 bits, so no product is truncated in the original either. The original sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is captured in the 16-bit corr_out_tmp, i.e. taken mod 2^16; taking result[15:0] of the block's exact sum gives the identical value.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are read only by the corr_out_tmp sum (corr: declared 1367-1368, used only at 1385; corr_seq: declared 1844-1845, used only at 1869). No individual product escapes either module, so both products may live inside the block. corr_out_tmp and corr_out keep their exact original values and timing, so everything above (wrapper_corr_*, wrapper_norm_corr_*, and the top-level sums at lines 316/368/400) is unaffected and is not edited.
  • In corr_seq the operand registers in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg (gated by new_data, lines 1850-1865) stay exactly as they are and feed the block; they are also read nowhere else.

Edit 1 — module corr (lines 1352-1387)

Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg (1367-1368) and the two multiply assignments (1374-1375). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),
        .result(sop_res));

and inside the existing always @(posedge clk) block put, in place of the two deleted multiply assignments:

verilog
            sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1385 to:

verilog
          corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;

Leave the new_data-gated corr_out <= corr_out_tmp (1377-1384) untouched. Leave the four unused wire declarations at 1363-1366 untouched.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = lrexrre_reg(t-1) + limxrim_reg(t-1) = (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im)(t-2) mod 2^16. New: sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = the same value. With all registers at 0 at power-up the two also agree on the first cycles: original corr_out_tmp(1) = 0 + 0 = 0, new corr_out_tmp(1) = sop_reg(0) = 0; and the new sop_reg(1) = f(inputs(0)) matches the original corr_out_tmp(2). The zero state maps to the zero state, so no start-up or post-reset divergence is introduced (corr has no reset port; rst reaches only top-level registers, so mid-run resets do not touch this pipeline).

Edit 2 — module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871)

Identical shape, but the block reads the already-registered operands. Delete lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (1844-1845) and the two multiplies (1867-1868). Add:

verilog
    reg[15:0] sop_reg;              // replaces lrexrre_reg / limxrim_reg
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

    comb_sop_2_18 u_sop (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re_reg}), .ay({11'b0, in_r_re_reg}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im_reg}), .by({11'b0, in_r_im_reg}),
        .result(sop_res));

Inside the existing always @(posedge clk), in place of the deleted multiplies:

verilog
             sop_reg <= sop_res[15:0];

and change line 1869 to corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;. The new_data-gated operand and corr_out assignments (1850-1865) are unchanged.

Equivalence: original corr_out_tmp(t) = f(in_*_reg(t-2)); new corr_out_tmp(t) = sop_reg(t-1) = sop_res(t-2)[15:0] = f(in_*_reg(t-2)). Same zero-state argument as above.

Register stages, before and after

  • corr: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 2 stages (product registers, then the sum register). After: 2 stages (sop_reg after the combinational block, then corr_out_tmp). The further new_data-gated stage into corr_out is unchanged, so the module's input-to-corr_out latency stays 3 gated/ungated stages exactly as before.
  • corr_seq: operands (module inputs) to corr_out_tmp — before: 3 stages (in_*_reg, product registers, sum register). After: 3 stages (in_*_reg, sop_reg, corr_out_tmp). Input-to-corr_out stays 4 stages.
  • No register changes anywhere else; the block itself has no registers and sits between two soft flip-flop stages in both modules (its inputs come from flip-flop outputs — sh_reg outputs for corr, in_*_reg for corr_seq — and its output is captured by sop_reg in the same cycle).

Registers removed/added

Per instance, two 16-bit registers are removed and one 16-bit register (sop_reg) is added: a net saving of 16 flip-flops × 76 instances. No added register's power-up value is relied upon for correctness beyond the zero-state mapping shown above, which holds for the original and the rewrite alike.

Candidates left unconverted

None. All four listed products are converted.

Resource check

76 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone, against 306 DSP tiles on the device. These are the only four multiply sites in the whole design (no other * operator exists in stereovision1.v, including in my_wrapper_divider), so nothing else competes for DSP tiles.

Instructions to the editor

  • Do not add a definition of comb_sop_2_18 to stereovision1.v; instantiate it only (the behavioural model is supplied as sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v).
  • Use the explicit zero-extending concatenations above rather than relying on implicit port widening.
  • Change nothing outside modules corr and corr_seq.

response

I'll read the source before deciding.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — no block is instantiated
PLAN:

Decision: no rewrite this pass. The block cannot win on area × delay for this design, and the measured pass confirms it quantitatively on both terms.

Every candidate region, and why it is left

All four candidates are left, for the same correctness-independent reason (the rewrite is correct — it was built and matched the reference simulation — but it is provably area-losing and gains no delay):

  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (64 instances)
  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (64 instances)
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (12 instances)
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (12 instances)

Reason, in full:

  1. Block count is fixed at one block per two products; it cannot be amortised. Every multiply in the design is 8×8 unsigned (sh_reg_w = 8, lines 1354–1360 and 1831–1837); corr computes in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im (1374–1375, summed at 1385) and corr_seq the same on its registered operands (1867–1869). That is exactly two product terms, so one comb_sop_2_18 per module instance — 76 blocks to convert everything, 12 if only corr_seq, 64 if only corr. Packing two instances into one block is not possible even though neighbouring correlators share the right-hand operands (out_r1/out_r2 are common to all six corr_seq instances of a wrapper, lines 1734–1739): separating two 16-bit products inside one 37-bit product requires the packed operand to be l_A·2^17 + l_B, i.e. 25 bits, and ax/bx are 18 bits. There is no 76-blocks-becomes-fewer-blocks rewrite available.
  2. The architecture packs the soft multipliers denser than the block does. At seed 1 the unmodified design uses 42 DSP tiles for all 152 of these 8×8 products, about 3.6 products per tile. A comb_sop_2_18 block takes its tile alone and holds 2 products — roughly half the density. Converting k module instances therefore costs about k − 2k/3.6 ≈ 0.45k extra DSP tiles, about 114 000 minimum-width transistor areas per instance, against a soft saving of only one 16-bit adder and a net 16 flip-flops per instance (~31 000 measured). The measured pass matches this arithmetic exactly: 76 blocks gave +34 DSP tiles and +24.3 % area. Scaling down does not change the sign — converting only corr_seq (the smallest step, 12 instances, since the instantiation is per module and reaches all of that module's instances) still predicts roughly +5 tiles, about +5 % area, and converting only corr about +29 tiles.
  3. There is no delay to win back. The converted region was not the baseline's critical path: the baseline period is 5.47292 ns while the original region's stages are a registered 8×8 multiply and a registered 16-bit add. After conversion the block path became the critical path at 5.61359 ns (FF clock-to-out 0.060, 2.150 wire into by[6], 2.140 through the block, 1.244 into $dff~1350). Even granting that the 2.150 ns and 1.244 ns routing terms shrink with fewer, better-placed blocks, the best this region can do is fall back under the untouched 5.47292 ns critical path elsewhere in the design — i.e. delay ratio 1.000, never below it. With D/D0 ≥ 1 and A/A0 > 1 for every possible conversion size, area × delay is above the baseline for every subset of these candidates.
  4. Nothing else in the design can use the block. These four sites are the only * operators in stereovision1.v, so there is no other region to try instead, and no regrouping across the 4-product tree helps: the tree is completed in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem by sign-extended 16-bit adds of whole corr_out values (lines 316–410), which are module outputs read by port_bus_1to0 and the wrappers, so those sums must stay outside any block, and absorbing them would not reduce the block count anyway.

Blocks added: 0. Register stages: unchanged everywhere, since nothing is converted (corr: 2 stages operands→corr_out_tmp, 3 to corr_out; corr_seq: 3 and 4).

The search space here is one dimension — how many of the 76 instances to convert — and both endpoints and every interior point are losing by the two independent arguments above, so there is nothing left to try. The run should end and keep the original design, since the only measured rewrite (+27.51 %) is above it.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The block would only pay off if each instantiation carried more arithmetic than two 8×8 products. Widening the correlator datapath (e.g. accumulating several taps' l·r terms per block, or computing the paired corr_out_1_pN + corr_out_1_nN sums of lines 316–410 from products rather than from already-registered 16-bit correlator outputs) would let one comb_sop_2_18 replace work that currently costs more than half a DSP tile of soft multiplier, which is the only way its 253 779-area tile-alone cost can be recovered on this architecture.

layout koios_small · used area 2.56155e+07 · critical path 5.47292 ns · wirelength 153334 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 1.40192e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 6.95%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 6.95%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

"Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants.
